    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.1

    ↗
    श्रीशुक उवाच पितरावुपलब्धार्थौ विदित्वा पुरुषोत्तम: । मा भूदिति निजां मायां ततान जनमोहिनीम् ॥ १ ॥

    Important Words
    • puruṣa - uttamaḥ — the Supreme Personality
      Puruṣottama — the supremely enjoying Person, distinct from kṣara and akṣara (BG 15.16-18). The very word used precisely here, while Kṛṣṇa is acting as a son, underlines that the child in Devakī's lap is the highest puruṣa Himself.
    • nijām — His personal
      Not borrowed, not external — Yogamāyā is Kṛṣṇa's own svarūpa-śakti. Gauḍīyas guard this carefully against any Māyāvāda reading that would make māyā an external veil; here it is Bhagavān's intimate potency acting for prema.
    • māyām — illusory potency
      Here specifically Yogamāyā, the internal energy that arranges līlā, not mahāmāyā that binds the conditioned. Viśvanātha Cakravartī notes she is set in motion so vātsalya may cover knowledge of His godhood.
    • jana — His devotees
      Viśvanātha glosses jana here as bhakta (citing diyamānaṁ na gṛhṇāti vinā mat-sevanaṁ janaḥ). The bewildering at issue is the loving forgetfulness given only to His own people, not the ignorance imposed on the godless.
    • mohinīm — which bewilders
      Feminine accusative — the potency personified. The same Mohinī-form by which Kṛṣṇa bewilders demons is here turned softly toward His parents so prema, not awe, may govern the relationship.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Understanding that His parents were becoming aware of His transcendental opulences, the Supreme Personality of Godhead thought that this should not be allowed to happen. Thus He expanded His Yoga-māyā, which bewilders His devotees.
    Purport

    If Vasudeva and Devakī would have seen Kṛṣṇa as almighty God, their intense love for Him as their son would have been spoiled. Lord Kṛṣṇa did not want this. Rather, the Lord wanted to enjoy with them the ecstatic love of vātsalya-rasa, the relationship between parents and children. As Śrīla Prabhupāda often pointed out, although we normally think of God as the supreme father, in Kṛṣṇa consciousness we can enter into the Lord’s pastimes and play the part of His parents, thus intensifying our love for Him. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura points out that the word jana may be translated here as “devotees,” as in the verse dīyamānaṁ na gṛhṇanti vinā mat-sevanaṁ janaḥ ( Bhāg. 3.29.13 ). He further explains that jana may also be translated as “parents,” since jana is derived from the verb jan, which in the causative form ( janayate ) means “to generate or to give birth to.” In this sense of the word (as in jananī or janakau ), the term jana-mohinī indicates that the Lord was about to expand His internal illusory potency so that Vasudeva and Devakī would again love Him as their dear child.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Understanding that His parents were becoming aware of His transcendental opulences, the Supreme Personality of Godhead thought that this should not be allowed to happen. Thus He expanded His Yogamāyā, which bewilders His devotees.
    Commentary

    In the forty fifth chapter, Krsna consoles Devaki and Vasudeva, sends Nanda and the cowherdmen back to Vraja, installs Ugrasena as king, goes to the gurukula and absorbs himself in study.

    Understanding that his mother and father were endowed with the treasure of seeing him as a figure of power, Krsna began to consider, "Let that perception vanish, and let parental love cover it, because by that both of us will attain supreme bliss." That is the meaning of upalabdhartha. He then spread out his yogamaya potency to cover knowledge of his godly powers. Jana in the phrase jana mohinim refers to the devotees as in the verse diyamanam na grhnati vina mat sevanam jana. Thus it refers to that maya whose nature is to bewilder the devotees. Another meaning of jana mohinim is that which bewilders his father and mother (janaka and janani mohinim). Sridhara Swami says in this regard, "Though I am pleased, is giving them such knowledge of me such a rare thing? What is rarer to give love of me as a son." In this way his intention is explained. Thus Krsna’s words in the next verses should not be considered deceptive, because, by that, he is invoking their parental love.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    From SB 10.44.38 (Kṛṣṇa dragged Kaṁsa’s dead body) until SB 10.45.2 (Kṛṣṇa spoke to his parents), one should discern the actual order of pastimes, just as there is importance in the order in the list starting with “Let various foods be cooked.” (SB 10.24.26) First he dragged Kaṁsa’s body on the ground so that people would believe that he was dead. Then he killed Kaṁsa’s brothers. Then he freed Vasudeva and Devakī. The parents then spoke to him with reverence. Since it was impossible for others to drag Kaṁsa’s body, huge as a mountain, to the bank of Yamunā, Kṛṣṇa did this personally. This dragging produced a channel in the ground. Viṣṇu Purāṇa says: gauraveṇātimahatā parikhā tena kṛṣyatā kṛtā kaṁsasya dehena vegeneva mahāmbhasaḥ Kaṁsa’s body produced a huge channel when Kṛṣṇa forcefully dragged it to the Yamunā. This is well known even today as Kaṁsa’s canal or Kaṁsa’s river. It joins the Yamunā at Viśrānta-tīrtha. This indicates the great size and hardness of Kaṁsa’s body. Then Kaṁsa’s wives came there and grieved. Then Kṛṣṇa saw that his parents understood his powers. Upalabdhārthau means that his parents attained a decrease (upa) in prema which recognized him as their son: they saw him as the Supreme Lord. Kṛṣṇa realized this after releasing them when they offered him respects. He thought, “This should not happen.” He then spread his māyā which bewilders people, which marks them attracted to him (nijām) as their son. yā prītir avivekānāṁ visayeṣv anapāyinī tvām anusmarataḥ sā me hṛdayān nāpasarpatu Unintelligent persons have unflinching affection for the objects of sense gratification. Similarly, may I always remember you, so that the same attachment, applied to you, never leaves my heart. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.20.19 Another meaning is “He spread his extraordinary mercy (māyām)” or that he spread mercy in the form of his parents’ vātsalya towards him. This mercy bewilders his devotees (jana-mohinīm). His devotees who saw him at that time and his devotees who hear about his pastimes now attain bewilderment due to prema. This statement indicates that such prema is the highest human goal. Śukadeva is also included in these devotees. Or he spread māyā which in some sense bewildered people. It was permissible to deceive his parents since it was previously justified in slightly different words. strīṣu narma-vivāhe ca vṛtty-arthe prāṇa-saṅkaṭe go-brāhmaṇārthe hiṁsāyāṁ nānṛtaṁ syāj jugupsitam In flattering a woman to bring her under control, in joking, in a marriage ceremony, in earning one’s livelihood, when one’s life is in danger, in protecting cows and brahminical culture, or in protecting a person from an enemy’s hand, falsity is never condemned. SB 8.19.43 This māyā made them, as parents, accept him as their son. Verse 10 describes the effect: they lifted him on their laps.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    I offer respects to the Lord’s power to impel jīvas with force, which could send Nanda back to Vraja.

    yasmāt kṣaram atīto ’ham akṣarād api cottamaḥ | ato ’smi loke vede ca prathitaḥ puruṣottamaḥ ||18|| Because I am superior to the conditioned and liberated jīvas, I am celebrated in the Vedas and the smṛtis as the Supreme Person. BG 15.18 As the supreme Lord (puruṣottamaḥ) he was omniscient and full of all qualities such as compassion. Knowing their awareness, he spread his power of eternity knowledge and bliss (māyām) or mercy, which bewilders his devotees, causing attachment to his lotus feet.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa draws a thin veil of Yogamāyā over Vasudeva and Devakī the way a lamp dims itself before a child whose eyes cannot bear the flame — not to hide, but so that vātsalya can come close. The same potency that throws non-devotees into ignorance gently covers His devotees' awe so they can hold Him as their son.

    Stories
    Yogamāyā at Yaśodā's lap

    Across Canto 10, the same Yogamāyā that forgets Brahmā's stolen calves (SB 10.13) and re-mothers Kṛṣṇa for Yaśodā (SB 10.8) operates here in Mathurā. The Bhāgavatam shows the pattern: aiśvarya (opulence) is repeatedly subordinated to mādhurya (sweetness) by Bhagavān's own internal potency, because rasa demands intimacy, and intimacy demands the cover of forgetfulness.

    Gauḍīya ācāryas read jana-mohinīm precisely this way — the māyā that bewilders His own people so that prema may flow as freely between Kṛṣṇa and His devotee as between any son and mother.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.2

    Important Words
    • sātvata - ṛṣabhaḥ — greatest of the Sātvatas
      Sātvata = the Yadu line, lineage of pure devotees; ṛṣabha = bull, foremost. Kṛṣṇa is here named not by His cosmic title but by His clan, anchoring Him in family — the necessary frame for what follows.
    • praśraya — with humility
      Praśraya is the affectionate humility a child offers a parent — distinct from danda-praṇāma to a superior. The word marks the rasa: Kṛṣṇa is not performing protocol, He is enacting filial love.
    • amba tāta iti — 'My dear mother, My dear father'
      The two address-particles that locate this entire chapter inside vātsalya-rasa. Gauḍīya ācāryas dwell on these syllables — that the Absolute consents to call two human beings 'Mom' and 'Dad' is the very nectar Yogamāyā has just made possible.
    • sa - ādaram — respectfully
      Sa-ādara = with ādara, deferential regard. The same Kṛṣṇa whom Brahmā and Indra had to bow to in earlier chapters here offers ādara to His parents — establishing the dharma of filial honor He is about to teach in verses 5-7.
    • prīṇan — gratifying them
      Present participle of √prī — to please, to satisfy lovingly. Bhagavān's first act after Kaṁsa's death is not coronation but prīṇana of His parents; the priorities of vātsalya are spelled out in a single participle.
    Lord Kṛṣṇa, the greatest of the Sātvatas, approached His parents with His elder brother. Humbly bowing His head and gratifying them by respectfully addressing them as “My dear mother” and “My dear father,” Kṛṣṇa spoke as follows.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Lord Kṛṣṇa, the greatest of the Sātvatas, approached His parents with His elder brother. Humbly bowing His head and gratifying them by respectfully addressing them as "My dear mother" and "My dear father," Kṛṣṇa spoke as follows.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “My dear father,” Kṛṣṇa spoke as follows. How he spread māyā is now described. After freeing them, he sent them home, and then came there. Then he spoke. He engaged others to perform the rites for Kaṁsa and others. He was the best of the Sātvatas: he must therefore show mercy to the Yadus who he had accepted as his family members. On seeing them, he began to show this mercy. Viṣṇu Purāṇa says mohāya yadu-cakrasya vitatāna sa vaiṣṇavīm: he spread yoga-māyā for bewildering the Yadus. He went with Balarāma in order to instil greater faith in his parents. By calling to them, he pleased them and becoming joyful himself, spoke with respect.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “My dear father,” Kṛṣṇa spoke as follows. His parents were standing at a distance out of devotion with knowledge that he was the Lord. Thus he approached them (etya). Or he came forward and embraced them, since he was the protector of devotes (sātvarṣabhaḥ). Or he appeared as the best of the Yadus to manifest unlimited qualities such as affection for the devotees. By giving mercy to his parents, he gave mercy to all the Yadus. Viṣṇu Purāṇa says mohāya yadu-cakrasya vitatāna sa vaiṣṇavīm: he spread yoga-māyā for bewildering the Yadus. He went with Balarāma in order to instil greater faith in his parents. By calling to them, he pleased them, and becoming joyful himself, he spoke with respect.

      His Holiness Bhakti Ashraya Vaishnava Swami· 2023-08-17· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma walking up to Vasudeva and Devakī and saying 'amba, tāta' is the universe addressing its source as 'Mom, Dad.' He who is approached by śruti through 'neti neti' here approaches His own parents bowing — because in vātsalya, the Lord shrinks Himself to be held.

    Stories
    Two sons returning together

    Throughout Canto 10, the pair-figure of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma — Bhagavān and His ādi-guru — moves as one bhāva. In Vraja They went to the forest together; here They return to Mathurā's parents together. Gauḍīyas read sa-agrajaḥ as a doctrinal note: even Bhagavān, when entering vātsalya, comes with the principle of guru-tattva at His side, never alone.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.3

    Important Words
    • bālya — toddler age
      The 1-5 year stage, when a child is held and fed. In Vraja, this is the entire register of Yaśodā's vātsalya — exactly what Devakī was robbed of by Kaṁsa's terror, and exactly what Kṛṣṇa now names.
    • paugaṇḍa — boyhood
      The 6-10 stage — calf-tending in Vraja-līlā. Gauḍīyas use these traditional age divisions (bālya, paugaṇḍa, kaiśora) to chart the development of Kṛṣṇa's pastimes and the corresponding rasas of His associates.
    • kaiśoraḥ — youth
      The 11-15 stage, the seat of mādhurya-rasa with the gopīs. Naming the three ages here is Kṛṣṇa Himself drawing the timeline that the rest of Canto 10's bhakti-rasa-śāstra (Rūpa Gosvāmī) will map.
    • utkaṇṭhitayoḥ — in anxiety
      Utkaṇṭhā — neck-craning, yearning. The same word is used for the gopīs' separation from Kṛṣṇa. In Gauḍīya rasa-theory, utkaṇṭhā is a constituent vibhāva of vipralambha (separation), and parents in separation share that current.
    • nitya — always
      Not occasionally — perpetually anxious. Kṛṣṇa is owning what every absent line of the Bhāgavatam carries by implication: Devakī never had a peaceful day under Kaṁsa.
    [Lord Kṛṣṇa said:] Dear Father, because of Us, your two sons, you and mother Devakī always remained in anxiety and could never enjoy Our childhood, boyhood or youth.
    Purport

    Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī discusses this verse as follows: “One may object that at this point Lord Kṛṣṇa had not actually passed the kaiśora stage [age ten to fifteen], since the women of Mathurā had stated, kva cāti-sukumārāṅgau kiśorau nāpta-yauvanau: ‘Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma have very tender limbs, being still at the kiśora stage, not having reached adolescence.’ ( Bhāg. 10.44.8 ) The definition of the different stages of growing up is given as follows: kaumāraṁ pañcamābdāntaṁ paugaṇḍaṁ daśamāvadhi kaiśoram ā-pañcadaśād yauvanaṁ tu tataḥ param ‘The kaumāra stage lasts until the age of five, paugaṇḍa up to age ten and kaiśora to age fifteen. From then on, one is known as yauvana. ’ According to this statement, the kaiśora period ends at the age of fifteen. Kṛṣṇa was only eleven years old when He killed Kaṁsa, according to Uddhava’s words: ekādaśa-samās tatra gūḍhārciḥ sa-balo ’vasat. ‘Like a covered flame, Lord Kṛṣṇa remained there incognito with Balarāma for eleven years.’ ( Bhāg. 3.2.26 ) And since Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma never took brahminical initiation in Vraja-bhūmi, it was at the time [of Their going to Mathurā] that Their kaiśora stage began rather than ended. “This objection to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s statement in the present verse — that His parents could not enjoy His kaiśora stage — is based on ordinary measurement of age. Yet we should consider the following statement: kālenālpena rājarṣe rāmaḥ kṛṣṇaś ca go-vraje aghṛṣṭa-jānubhiḥ padbhir vicakramatur añjasā ‘O King Parīkṣit, within a short time Rāma and Kṛṣṇa began to walk very easily in Gokula on Their legs, by Their own strength, without the need to crawl.’ Sometimes we see that the son of a king, even while in his paugaṇḍa stage of life, undergoes exceptional physical growth and exhibits activities appropriate to a kaiśora. Then what to speak of Lord Kṛṣṇa, whose exceptional growth is established in the Vaiṣṇava-toṣaṇī, Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu, Ānanda-vṛndavana-campū and other works? “The three years and four months that Lord Kṛṣṇa stayed in Mahāvana were the equivalent of five years for an ordinary child, and thus in that period He completed His kaumāra stage of childhood. The period from then to the age of six years and eight months, during which He lived in Vṛndāvana, constitutes His paugaṇḍa stage. And the period from the age of six years and eight months through His tenth year, during which time He lived in Nandīśvara [Nandagrāma], constitutes His kaiśora stage. Then, at the age of ten years and seven months, on the eleventh lunar day of the dark fortnight of the month of Caitra, He went to Mathurā, and on the fourteenth day thereafter He killed Kaṁsa. Thus He completed His kaiśora period at age ten, and He eternally remains at that age. In other words, we should understand that from this point on the Lord remains forever a kiśora. ” Thus Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī analyzes the intricacies of this verse.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    [Lord Kṛṣṇa said:] Dear Father, because of Us, your two sons, you and mother Devakī always remained in anxiety and could never enjoy Our childhood, boyhood or youth.
    Commentary

    "Because of your anxiety for us, you never had the opportunity of happiness of raising us through the balya, pauganda and kaisora ages." "But the women of Mathura mentioned in chapter 44, verse 8 that Krsna and Balarama were of kaisora age, and had not reached yauvana. Thus the match between the huge wrestlers and the young boys was unequal. Therefore it cannot be said that they had finished their kaisora age. Kaumara age is up to the fifth year. Pauganda is the fifth year to the tenth year. Kaisora is from the tenth to fifteenth year. After that is yauvana. As Krsna was eleven when he killed Kamsa, he would be in the kairsora age according to the above definition. Uddhava has said, "Krsna lived with Balarama secretly in Vraja until the eleventh year." Because of this statement, and the fact that they had not undergone the upanayana samskara, it should be concluded that they started the kaisora age while in Vraja but it had not been completed when they left." "That is true, if you take the normal method of counting the years. However chapter 8, verse.26 states that Krsna and Balarama in a very short time began to crawl about. It is sometimes seen that a young prince during pauganda age may show symptoms of the kaisora age with his quick bodily growth. Therefore what to speak of Krsna attaining kaisora symptoms during pauganda age? Therefore in the case of Krsna, the ages are established according to the following norm following the Vaisnava Tosani, Bhaktirasamrta sindhu and Ananda vrndavana campu. At the age of three years and four months, Krsna displayed the qualities of a five year old. During this kaumara age, Krsna stayed in Mahavana. From that time till the age of six years and eight months, during his pauganda age, he stayed in Vrndavana. From six years and eight months until his tenth year he experienced kaisora age while living in Nandisvara. In the seventh month of the tenth year during the month of Caitra on the krsna trayodasi he went to Mathura and on the caturdasi he killed Kamsa. Therefore the tenth year was the end of his kaisora age, but thereafter he remained at the same age. That is illustrated by the following statement: "When the kaisora age Pradyumna entered the apartments of Krsna, he appeared the same as Krsna and the women hid themselves out of shame." (SB10.55.28). There are also similar statements in the agamas. Thus on the day of killing Kamsa, Krsna had both passed the kaisora age and not passed it. By this explanation there is harmony between Krsna’s statement in this verse that kaisora age passed, and the statement of the women of Mathura that he was in his kaisora age.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He addresses his father first, since he is more prominent. He addresses him as father to arouse within him paternal affection since Vasudeva did not have strong affection, because he had knowledge of Kṛṣṇa as the Lord. In saying that they could not enjoy his kaiśora period, he means that they could not enjoy his full kaiśora period, but only part. He does not mean to say that he has now passed the kaiśora period since it is said kaiśorau nāpa yavanau: they attained kaiśora period, but did not enter yauvana period. They had just begun their kaiśora period. One should consider the order of their age. According to the people of Vraja he lifted Govardhana when he was seven years old. kva sapta-hāyano bālaḥ kva mahādri-vidhāraṇam tato no jāyate śaṅkā vraja-nātha tavātmaje On the one hand this boy is only seven years old, and on the other we see that he has lifted the great hill Govardhana. Therefore, O King of Vraja, a doubt about your son arises within us. SB 10.26.14 He held up the mountain on the third lunar day during the waxing moon of Kārtika month. He completed the seventh year on the eighth lunar day of gauṇa Bhādra month. Out of affection the people stated that he was seven years old, when actually he was seven years, two months and ten days old. Taking that date as a marker, one should calculate the age at which he performed the previous pastimes. One should also consider the following statement and the example of quick development seen in a prince: kālenālpena rājarṣe rāmaḥ kṛṣṇaś ca gokule aghṛṣṭa-jānubhiḥ padbhir vicakramatur aṣjasā O King Parīkṣit, within a very short time both Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa began to walk very easily in Gokula on their legs, by their own strength, without the need to crawl. SB 10.8.26 Therefore it was not impossible that Kṛṣṇa lifted Govardhana when a little over seven years old, since he was moreover, the Supreme Lord. He killed Tṛnāvarta when he was one year old. Dāmodara pastime took place when at the beginning of his third year, during Kārtika month. Some days after that, they went from Gokula to Vṛndāvana. After two or three months Kṛṣṇa began herding the calves. Whiling herding the calves he killed Vatsāsura, Bakāṣura and Vyomāsura. When the third year was complete and he entered his fourth year, in the autumn season Brahmā sole the calves and cowherd boys. He entered paugaṇḍa period at the time. However it is said: brahman kālāntara-kṛtaṁ tat-kālīnaṁ kathaṁ bhavet yat kaumāre hari-kṛtaṁ jaguḥ paugaṇḍake ’rbhakāḥ O great sage, how could things done in the past have been described as being done at the present? Kṛṣṇa performed this pastime of killing Aghāsura during his kaumāra age. How then, during his paugaṇḍa age, could the boys have described this incident as having happened recently? SB 10.12.41 Thus, because of the theft, there was delay in paugaṇḍa manifesting. Paugaṇḍa age appeared when Kṛṣṇa was five years old. He began herding the cows on the eighth lunar day of the waxing moon in Kārtika month. In the summer of the fifth year he subdued Kāliya. In his sixth year he had fun herding the cows. At the beginning of the seventh year he entered kaiśora period. At that time he killed Dhenuka on going to the forest of ripe tāla fruits. That evening, the gopīs showed first signs of love for him: pītvā mukunda-mukha-sāragham akṣi-bhṛṅgais tāpaṁ jahur viraha-jaṁ vraja-yoṣito ’hni tat sat-kṛtiṁ samadhigamya viveśa goṣṭhaṁ savrīḍa-hāsa-vinayaṁ yad apāṅga-mokṣam With their beelike eyes, the women of Vṛndāvana drank the honey of the beautiful face of Mukunda, and thus they gave up the distress they had felt during the day because of separation from Him. The young Vṛndāvana ladies cast sidelong glances at the Lord—glances filled with bashfulness, laughter and submission—and Kṛṣṇa, completely accepting these glances as a proper offering of respect, entered the cowherd village. SB 10.15.43 The pastimes of subduing Kāliya and killing Dhenuka are narrated out of order. In the summer of the seventh year Pralamba was killed. During the Āśvina month of his eighth year he played the flute. During Kārtika month he lifted Govardhana. This is when the inhabitants exclaimed that he was only seven years old. tato nanda-vrajam itaḥ pitrā kaṁsād vibibhyatā ekādaśa samās tatra gūḍhārciḥ sa-balo ’vasat His father, afraid of Kaṁsa, brought him to Vraja and for eleven years he lived there with Balarāma, like a covered flame. SB 3.2.26 At the beginning of his eighth year, on the eleventh day of Kārtika, waxing moon, he was bathed and given the name Govinda. On the twelfth day, he went to Varuṇa’s kingdom. On the full moon day he immersed the inhabitants in Brahma-hrada. During the winter season, he stole the gopīs’ clothing, since this is mentioned in Varāha Purāṇa. In the summer of his eighth year he gave mercy to the wives of the brāhmaṇas. In his ninth year he performed the rāsa dance. He went to Ambikā-vana during Śiva-rātri, on the fourteenth lunar day. He killed Śaṅkhacūḍa in Phālguna month. In his tenth year he performed pastimes as he pleased. He killed Ariṣṭa on the full moon of Caitrya month when he was eleven. He killed Keśī on the twelfth lunar day of gauṇa Phālguna. On the fourteenth day he killed Kaṁsa. He was not actually twelve years old at that time, but eleven as mentioned in SB 3.2.26. At the end of his ninth year he entered full kaiśora period. He does not age beyond this period. kṛṣṇaṁ matvā striyo hrītā nililyus tatra tatra ha The women of the palace thought Pradyumna was Kṛṣṇa. Thus the women became bashful and hid themselves here and there. SB 10.55.28 Though Pradyumna was young, he was similar to Kṛṣṇa.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He addresses his father first, since he is more prominent. He addresses him as father to arouse within him paternal affection since Vasudeva did not have strong affection, because he had knowledge of Kṛṣṇa as the Lord. In saying that they could not enjoy his kaiśora period, he means that they could not enjoy his full kaiśora period, but only the very end.

    Ananda
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa lists the missed years — bālya, paugaṇḍa, kaiśora — the way a son returning from long exile counts birthdays he could not attend. Bhagavān, who is beyond time, performs the grammar of regret so His parents' grief has a place to land.

    Important Words
    • daiva - hatayoḥ — deprived by fate
      Daiva = providence; hata = struck down. Kṛṣṇa, who Himself is the controller of daiva, here speaks as a son whose family was struck by it — the maximum tenderness Yogamāyā permits Him.
    • vāsaḥ — residence
      Not visiting but vāsa — dwelling. Vāsa-bhakti, dwelling-with the Lord, is precisely what Vasudeva and Devakī were denied and what Nanda and Yaśodā received in Gokula. The single noun encodes the whole asymmetry of Canto 10.
    • lālitāḥ — pampered
      Past participle of √lāl — to fondle, indulge. The Vraja-vātsalya verb par excellence; cf. SB 10.8 where Yaśodā lālayati her bound son. Kṛṣṇa is using Vraja's word to describe what Mathurā lost.
    • mudam — happiness
      Muda — not sukha (comfort) but glad-heartedness. The specific joy of a coddled child, distinct from the joys of opulence Mathurā could supply. Kṛṣṇa is naming the rasa, not the resource.
    • pitṛ - geha — parents' home
      Pitṛ-geha is a recognized śāstric locus — the natural setting for the child's saṁskāras. Kṛṣṇa frames the deprivation in dharma's own vocabulary, so His parents' grief is sanctioned, not merely sentimental.
    Deprived by fate, We could not live with you and enjoy the pampered happiness most children enjoy in their parents’ home.
    Purport

    Here Lord Kṛṣṇa points out that not only did His parents suffer in separation from Him and Balarāma, but the two boys also suffered in separation from Their parents.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Deprived by fate, We could not live with you and enjoy the pampered happiness most children enjoy in their parents' home.
    Commentary

    "We also have been unfortunate (daiva hatayoh)." However the words also mean the opposite: "We have attained good fortune in not attaining the happiness of ordinary children."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Not only was your happiness destroyed, but ours also. We were excluded from happiness as sons. Or we did not have the opportunity to give you happiness, being useless sons. Thus all our happiness was destroyed. They then speak this verse. We, dependent on bad karma (adaiva-hatayoḥ), did not live with you. Children, living in their parents’ house, cared for by their parents, experience happiness. Or, children living in their parents’ house naturally experience happiness and are particularly cared for by them.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    We should not have deprived you of happiness, but we were deprived of happiness. By ill fate we could not live with you. He says this with great humility. However his words can also mean “Not affected by fate (adaiva-hatayoḥ), we could not live with you.” The Lord is not dependent on karma. Children, living in their parents’ house, cared for by their parents ( or even not cared for alalitā), experience happiness.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A child fed in a stranger's house can be safe and even cherished, but the lap of one's own parents is a different geography altogether. Kṛṣṇa names this geography to soothe Devakī — He knows what was taken, and He is calling it by its name.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.5

    Important Words
    • sarva - artha - sambhavaḥ — source of all goals of life
      The body is the upādhi by which dharma, artha, kāma and mokṣa can be pursued. Gauḍīyas add a fifth — prema — and note that even bhakti requires the human body the parents gave.
    • janitaḥ — born
      From √jan — the very root of jana (verse 1) and jananī (verse 22). The semantic web throughout this chapter binds Kṛṣṇa's parents, His devotees, and birth itself into one cluster of dependence on śakti.
    • poṣitaḥ — maintained
      From √puṣ — to nourish. The verb of vātsalya; cf. Yaśodā's poṣaṇa of Kṛṣṇa in SB 10.8. Body without poṣaṇa cannot serve the higher arthas, which is why the debt is double — janma plus poṣaṇa.
    • nirveśam — repayment of the debt
      Nirveśa = settlement, discharge. The classical term for clearing the three ṛṇas. Kṛṣṇa is teaching that this particular ṛṇa is structurally unclearable — which sets up His own apology in verse 9.
    • śata - āyuṣā — with a hundred-year life
      The Vedic full life-span (jīved śaradaḥ śatam). Even at maximum allotted time, the debt cannot be cleared — meaning the duty is one of bhāva, not arithmetic. Service is owed lifelong because gratitude has no terminal payment.
    With one’s body one can acquire all goals of life, and it is one’s parents who give the body birth and sustenance. Therefore no mortal man can repay his debt to his parents, even if he serves them for a full lifetime of a hundred years.
    Purport

    Having stated, “Both you, Our parents, and We have suffered because of Our separation,” Kṛṣṇa now states that His and Balarāma’s religious principles have been spoiled by Their failure to satisfy Their parents.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    With one's body one can acquire all goals of life, and it is one's parents who give the body birth and sustenance. Therefore no mortal man can repay his debt to his parents, even if he serves them for a full lifetime of a hundred years.
    Commentary

    "One cannot repay the debt to the parents who give birth to the body from which arises all aspirations such as dharma."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The body is born and nourished by parents. This is a different reason for offering respect to parents. However, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma were not nourished by Vasudeva and Devakī. This verse cannot apply to them.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The body is usually nourished by the parents. However, Kṛṣṇa was nourished by Nanda and Yaśodā. The body is subject to death (martyaḥ) and lives only a hundred years.

      His Holiness Sacinandana Swami· 2021-09-04· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Bhagavān turns the dharma of pitṛ-ṛṇa back upon Himself like a king who chooses to stand in the petitioner's line. He owes nothing, yet He bows and says, 'A man cannot repay his parents in a hundred years' — teaching the gṛhastha-dharma from the inside.

    Stories
    Pitṛ-ṛṇa in the dharma-śāstra tradition

    The three debts — to the ṛṣis, to the devas, to the pitṛs — are foundational to Vedic adult life (taittirīya, manu-smṛti). Among them pitṛ-ṛṇa is unique: it is owed to those who gave the body itself, the upādhi by which any other dharma can be performed.

    Kṛṣṇa here recites this ancient teaching not as a debtor but as ācārya. The Bhāgavatam pattern is consistent: Bhagavān keeps the standards of dharma in His own conduct (cf. SB 1.9, Bhīṣma's praise of Kṛṣṇa for sva-dharma-rakṣaṇa) so that householders have a model to follow.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Important Words
    • ātma - jaḥ — a son
      Literally 'self-born' — the child is the parent's own ātman extended. The compound implies that refusing parents is a kind of self-refusal, which is why the karmic recoil is sva-māṁsa-khādana.
    • kalpaḥ — capable
      The condition matters: dharma applies to those with means. Kṛṣṇa is careful — He is not condemning the helpless poor but the able who choose otherwise.
    • vṛttim — a livelihood
      Vṛtti = sustenance, support. The minimum a capable child owes — not luxury, but the means of living. Gauḍīya gṛhasthas like Sanātana Gosvāmī before renunciation took this baseline obligation seriously even while preparing for tyāga.
    • pretya — after passing away
      After death — the karma matures post-mortem in pretaloka. Kṛṣṇa is using standard śāstric eschatology to land a domestic duty; bhakti does not abolish dharma's natural retribution.
    • sva - māṁsam — his own flesh
      The visceral image is intentional. Manu-smṛti uses similar formulae; Kṛṣṇa is quoting the tradition's own iron rhetoric to make the duty inescapable. Sva-māṁsa-khādana = the karmic boomerang of consuming one's source.
    A son who, though able to do so, fails to provide for his parents with his physical resources and wealth is forced after his death to eat his own flesh.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    A son who, though able to do so, fails to provide for his parents with his physical resources and wealth is forced after his death to eat his own flesh.
    Commentary

    "The son who, though able , does not support his parents according to the rules of the scriptures on the path of karma, has his flesh eaten by the servants of Yama after death and before getting a new body."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The servants of Yama make him eat his own flesh.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Physical resources and wealth should both be given (ca). He must eat his own (svasya) flesh. Hi means certainly. If one is ill, they rule does not apply.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Anahankarah (Freedom from False Ego)Kshama (Forgiveness / Patience)Karuna (Kindness / Compassion)Tyaga (Renunciation / Letting Go)Bhakti (Devotion)Vinaya (Humility / Modesty)Prema (Divine Love)Samata (Equanimity / Even-mindedness)
    Related verses
    BG 12.13 BG 2.62 BG 3.11 BG 12.3 BG 7.8 BG 6.1 BG 16.4 BG 15.1 BG 6.4 BG 2.60
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A son with the means who refuses his aging parents is, in the śāstra's stark image, made to eat his own flesh hereafter — because he is already eating it now, only the chewing is invisible. Kṛṣṇa quotes the heavy verse so the dharma can land on every gṛhastha listening over the householders' shoulders.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.7

    ↗
    मातरं पितरं वृद्धं भार्यां साध्वीं सुतं शिशुम् । गुरुं विप्रं प्रपन्नं च कल्पोऽबिभ्रच्छ्वसन् मृत: ॥ ७ ॥
    mātaraṁ pitaraṁ vṛddhaṁ

    Important Words
    • mātaram — mother
      Listed first — even before pitā in this verse's order. In dharma-śāstra mātṛ-devo bhava (Taittirīya) is the primary upāsanā for the householder. Kṛṣṇa is grounding the entire chapter's apology to Devakī in this priority.
    • bhāryām sādhvīm — chaste wife
      Bhāryā = one to be supported (bhṛ); sādhvī = chaste. The compound sets the standard — the dharma of maintaining wife presumes her chastity-bhakti, just as her chastity presumes his support. Mutual.
    • gurum — a spiritual master
      Listed alongside mother and father — Gauḍīyas treat guru as a third parent, the dīkṣā-pitṛ. Foreshadowing the gurukula episode that closes this very chapter (verses 30-48).
    • prapannam — one who has come for shelter
      From pra-√pad — to fall forward, to surrender. The technical term of śaraṇāgati. Bhagavān Himself promises in BG 18.66 to protect the prapanna; refusing one is to refuse the dharma Bhagavān keeps.
    • śvasan - mṛtaḥ — breathing dead
      Compound — breathing yet dead. A live corpse. Kṛṣṇa's diagnosis is severe: failure of these seven duties hollows the body out, leaving only respiration without a living dharma inside.
    A man who, though able to do so, fails to support his elderly parents, chaste wife, young child or spiritual master, or who neglects a brāhmaṇa or anyone who comes to him for shelter, is considered dead, though breathing.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    A man who, though able to do so, fails to support his elderly parents, chaste wife, young child or spiritual master, or who neglects a brāhmaṇa or anyone who comes to him for shelter, is considered dead, though breathing.
    Commentary

    "One who does not support (abibhrat) mother, father, etc. though living, is considered dead."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Vṛddham means the elders of the family. A man, though capable (kalpaḥ), who fails to support his elderly parents, is considered dead, though he still breathes. If he is incapable, the effects are less.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He should support elders, or an old father and a chaste wife. Gurus and brāhmaṇas should always be supported (even if not old). Wives etc. are mentioned, though not related to the case at hand. Those capable are at fault by not supporting them. If not capable, the fault does not apply. Thus Vasudeva and Devakī are not at fault since they were incapable.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Dana (Charity / Generosity)Svadhyaya (Self-study & Scripture Study)Guru Bhakti (Reverence for Teacher)Shraddha (Faith / Reverence)Aparigraha (Non-possessiveness)Nishkama Karma (Selfless Action)Seva (Service / Selfless Action)Yajña (Sacrifice / Spirit of Offering)
    Related verses
    BG 17.14 BG 3.9 BG 4.8 BG 4.34 BG 12.5 BG 13.8 BG 3.21 BG 6.1 BG 3.13
    Concepts
    GuruJiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A breathing man who fails to provide for mother, father, chaste wife, infant child, guru, brāhmaṇa, or refugee is, śāstra says, already a corpse. Kṛṣṇa lists the seven the way a physician lists the pulses — if any one is missing, the body that walks is not alive.

    Stories
    Daśaratha and the protected refugee

    In the Rāmāyaṇa, King Daśaratha holds the principle prapanna-rakṣaṇa so strictly that he sends his beloved Rāma to the forest rather than break a vow given to Kaikeyī as refuge for her wishes. The ideal kṣatriya treats the prapanna as inviolable even at the cost of his own life.

    Kṛṣṇa, listing prapannam in this verse alongside mother and guru, locates the refuge-seeker among the dearmost dependents — a Gauḍīya touchstone, because śaraṇāgati (taking refuge in Bhagavān) presumes a Lord who Himself honors that bond toward those who take refuge in His servants.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.8

    ↗
    तन्नावकल्पयो: कंसान्नित्यमुद्विग्नचेतसो: । मोघमेते व्यतिक्रान्ता दिवसा वामनर्चतो: ॥ ८ ॥
    tan nāv akalpayoḥ kaṁsān
    [tan naav akalpayoh kamsaan]
    nityam udvigna-cetasoḥ
    [nityam udvigna-chetasoh]

    [mogham ete vyatikraantaa]

    [divasaa vaam anarchatoh]

    tat — therefore nau — of Us two akalpayoḥ — who were unable kaṁsāt — because of Kaṁsa nityam — always udvigna — disturbed cetasoḥ — whose minds mogham — uselessly ete — these vyatikrāntāḥ — spent divasāḥ — days vām — you anarcatoḥ — not honoring .

    Important Words
    • akalpayoḥ — who were unable
      Echo of kalpaḥ in verse 6 — the same root, now negated. Verse 6 condemned the capable who do not act; here Kṛṣṇa places Himself among the incapable, because Kaṁsa's terror removed the conditions for filial service.
    • udvigna - cetasoḥ — whose minds were disturbed
      Udvigna — agitated, anxious. The same word Arjuna uses for his battlefield distress. Kṛṣṇa is acknowledging that even He, in His human-līlā, lived under the duress of Kaṁsa's threat.
    • mogham — uselessly
      Wasted, in vain. From the same root as moha. The days were 'wasted' in the strict sense that arcana of parents could not be done — by the standard of vātsalya-dharma the years were void.
    • vyatikrāntāḥ — spent
      Crossed over, gone past — irretrievable. Time has a one-directional grammar even for Bhagavān within līlā; the apology is to that very irreversibility.
    • anarcatoḥ — not honoring
      From an + √arc — failing to perform arcana (worship). Parents are listed among the daily arcana objects in smārta dharma. Kṛṣṇa frames missed sonship as missed pūjā — making the apology liturgical, not merely emotional.
    Thus We have wasted all these days, unable as We were to properly honor you because Our minds were always disturbed by fear of Kaṁsa.
    Purport

    Lord Kṛṣṇa continues to bring Vasudeva and Devakī back to their normal parental feelings toward Him and Balarāma. An ordinary child would be afraid of a cruel, tyrannical king like Kaṁsa, and Lord Kṛṣṇa here plays the part of such a child, thus evoking the parental sympathy of Vasudeva and Devakī.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Thus We have wasted all these days, unable as We were to properly honor you because Our minds were always disturbed by fear of Kaṁsa.
    Commentary

    "Therefore because we were unable to serve you for fear of Kamsa (kamsat udvigna cetasoh) , our days have passed uselessly. We are at fault." But the real meaning is, "We spent all these days uselessly, our minds wanting go to Mathura, because we heard about Kamsa and wanted to fight him (udvigna cetasoh), but were unable to go." In this case udvigna means moving or going (vij). When Krsna says he spend his days uselessly it is an expression of regret.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    In this verse they speak of their own condition. They were incapable of providing support because of Kaṁsa. We wasted our days since we did not worship you (vām).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    We could not honor you because of fear of Kaṁsa. Thus our kaiśora days have been spent uselessly.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Anahankarah (Freedom from False Ego)Dana (Charity / Generosity)Prema (Divine Love)
    Related verses
    BG 9.1 BG 4.8 BG 15.20 BG 15.7 BG 2.62 BG 3.11 BG 12.3 BG 16.1 BG 3.19 BG 3.30 BG 7.1 BG 2.66 BG 12.5 BG 2.60 BG 13.8 BG 7.8
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa describes His Mathurā years as 'days spent uselessly without honoring you' the way a son returning home counts every postcard he never sent. The Lord of all sacrifices apologizes for missed arcana — letting His parents weep over what was, before He reveals what is.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.9

    ↗

    Important Words
    • kṣantum — to forgive
      Infinitive of √kṣam — to bear, to forgive. Kṣamā is itself a quality of saints (BG 16.3); Bhagavān asks His parents to exercise it on Him. The role-reversal is the verse's heart.
    • para - tantrayoḥ — under the control of others
      Para = other; tantra = control. The opposite of svatantra (independent). Bhagavān, the only truly svatantra-tattva (cf. SB 1.1.1, janmādy asya yataḥ), declares Himself para-tantra — vātsalya's grammar.
    • śuśrūṣām — service
      From śuśrūṣā, the desiderative of √śru — the desire to hear, to attend upon. The classical word for personal service to elders and gurus. Foreshadows verse 32 where Kṛṣṇa renders śuśrūṣā at Sāndīpani's gurukula.
    • durhṛdā — by the hardhearted
      Dur + hṛd — bad-hearted, malicious. Kaṁsa is named only by epithet here; the verse keeps his name off the lips during the apology so the rasa stays clean.
    • kliṣṭayoḥ — (Us) caused pain
      From √kliś — to torment. Same root as kleśa (the five afflictions of yoga). Bhagavān, who is sat-cit-ānanda, accepts the predicate kliṣṭa within His līlā so that His parents' historical anguish receives Lord-side acknowledgment.
    Dear Father and Mother, please forgive Us for not serving you. We are not independent and have been greatly frustrated by cruel Kaṁsa.
    Purport

    According to Sanskrit grammar, the words para-tantrayoḥ and kliṣṭayoḥ may also refer to Vasudeva and Devakī. Actually, Vasudeva and Devakī were under the control of Providence and were disturbed by the activities of Kaṁsa, whereas Śrī Kṛṣṇa is always the absolute Personality of Godhead.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Dear Father and Mother, please forgive Us for not serving you. We are not independent and have been greatly frustrated by cruel Kaṁsa.
    Commentary

    "Please forgive us (nauh). We could not serve you, who were dependent upon evil minded Kamsa, and yet were tormented by him."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You should forgive us for not serving you (tat). He addresses them as father and mother again in order to arouse affection in them. Since you are our father and mother, you should forgive us for not serving you since we were dependent--living in hiding and afflicted by evil Kaṁsa (duhṛdā).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    They call out to their parents to invoke their affection. Forgive us for not being able to serve you, since we were not independent. The parents should forgive the offense of the children out of affection. Moreover we had to hide and were persecuted by Kaṁsa.

    Related verses

    • Tamohara Pr SB 10-45-09-14 - 2014-06-25 ISKCON Alachua
      His Grace Tamohara Prabhu· 2014-06-25· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Bhagavān asks His own parents for kṣamā the way a guest asks the host for forgiveness for being delayed by a storm — except the storm here was Kaṁsa, and the Guest is the Self of all selves. The asking is the gift; Yogamāyā lets them hear it as their boy speaking.

    Stories
    Para-tantra in Bhagavān's līlā

    Throughout Canto 10, Bhagavān repeatedly displays para-tantratā — submission to the situation His own līlā has set up. He runs from Jarāsandha (SB 10.50, 'Raṇacchora'), He is bound by Yaśodā (SB 10.9), He is rebuked by Satrājit (SB 10.56). Gauḍīya ācāryas read these as the deepest aiśvarya of all: He whose svātantrya is absolute consents to be para-tantra in prema.

    Here Kṛṣṇa says He is para-tantra under cruel Kaṁsa — not in truth, but in the rasa-world He inhabits as Devakī's son. The forgiveness He asks is the forgiveness only a child can ask.

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.10

    ↗
    श्रीशुक उवाच इति मायामनुष्यस्य हरेर्विश्वात्मनो गिरा । मोहितावङ्कमारोप्य परिष्वज्यापतुर्मुदम् ॥ १० ॥

    Important Words
    • māyā - manuṣyasya — of Him who appears as a human by His potency
      Crucial Gauḍīya compound. Bhagavān's manuṣya-rūpa is real but is sustained by His own māyā (= Yogamāyā) — not material illusion. He is not 'pretending' to be human; He is genuinely playing human inside His own potency.
    • hareḥ — of Lord Śrī Hari
      Hari = He who removes (sin, sorrow, the heart itself). The name placed precisely where Devakī and Vasudeva embrace Him — He is taking away their long anxiety in the very act of being held.
    • viśva - ātmanaḥ — the Soul of the universe
      He is simultaneously the universal Self and the lap-child. Gauḍīyas use such juxtapositions to teach acintya-bhedābheda — Bhagavān's vibhūti is non-different from Him, yet His personal form is distinct and primary.
    • aṅkam — upon their laps
      The lap as bhakti's destination (see story). Single noun loaded with all of vātsalya-rasa.
    • pariṣvajya — embracing
      Pari + √svaj — to embrace fully, to encircle. The same verb returns in verses 20 and 25 for Nanda. The whole chapter is structured by serial pariṣvajya-s — parental embraces forming the chapter's spine.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Thus beguiled by the words of Lord Hari, the Supreme Soul of the universe, who by His internal illusory potency appeared to be a human, His parents joyfully raised Him up on their laps and embraced Him.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Thus beguiled by the words of Lord Hari, the Supreme Soul of the universe, who by His internal illusory potency appeared to be a human, His parents joyfully raised Him up on their laps and embraced Him.
    Commentary

    Bewildered by such words of Krsna, who was deceptively appearing as a human being (maya manusya), they embraced him.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    His parents forgot everything except their vātsalya because he was the parabrahman in human form, with predominance of mercy (māyā-manuṣyasya), because he was attractive to all with unequalled sweetness (hareḥ), and because he was the shelter of unconditional prema for the whole universe, as Paramātmā (viśvātmanaḥ). They raised the two boys on their laps and embraced them.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The Lord was antaryāmī, (viśvātmānaḥ), with human form by his mercy (māyā-manuṣyasya), attractive to the world (hareḥ). Or he had a human form, attractive to all the world, because he was the dearest (viśvātmanaḥ). By his words he attracted their hearts (mohitau). They placed Kṛṣṇa or both of them on their laps, with more affection for Kṛṣṇa.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Vasudeva and Devakī sweep Kṛṣṇa onto their laps like two travelers who, after a long famine, finally taste their own home's rice. He who lives within every heart as Viśvātman makes Himself small enough to be cradled — the highest reciprocation of vātsalya bhakti.

    Stories
    Lap as Kṛṣṇa's destination

    Across Canto 10 the aṅka (lap) is a named śāstric locus: Pūtanā tried to claim it with poison (SB 10.6); Yaśodā filled it with milk (SB 10.7-8); Brahmā in his cosmic form could only bow at its feet (SB 10.13-14). Here in Mathurā, Devakī finally gets her aṅka filled. The whole canto reads as a long itinerary of where Kṛṣṇa lets Himself be placed.

    Gauḍīyas read the aṅka as bhakti's truest pīṭha — higher than any throne, because the throne expects His opulence whereas the lap expects only Him.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.11

    ↗
    सिञ्चन्तावश्रुधाराभि: स्‍नेहपाशेन चावृतौ । न किञ्चिदूचतू राजन्बाष्पकण्ठौ विमोहितौ ॥ ११ ॥
    siñcantāv aśru-dhārābhiḥ

    Important Words
    • aśru - dhārābhiḥ — with showers of tears
      Aśru-dhārā = streams of tears. In Gauḍīya rasa-śāstra, aśru is one of the eight sāttvika-bhāvas — bodily symptoms of mature prema. The parents are not weeping from grief but from the breach of long-restrained love.
    • sneha - pāśena — by the rope of affection
      Sneha is the technical Gauḍīya term for one specific rung in vātsalya's ladder (sneha → māna → praṇaya → rāga → anurāga → bhāva → mahābhāva). The chapter quietly uses the proper rasa-vocabulary.
    • bāṣpa - kaṇṭhau — whose throats were full of tears
      Bāṣpa = teardrops; kaṇṭha = throat. The throat-block of stambha/svara-bheda, classical sāttvika lakṣaṇas. Their silence is not absence of words but presence of too much bhāva for words.
    • vimohitau — overwhelmed
      Vi + √muh — completely bewildered (by sneha). The same root as the Yogamāyā of verse 1 (mohinīm). Their bewilderment is Yogamāyā's gift, not its curse.
    • āvṛtau — enveloped
      Past participle of ā + √vṛ — covered, wrapped. They are wrapped in sneha as a baby is wrapped in cloth — the verb makes the rope of verse 11 into a swaddling-cloth of love.
    Pouring out a shower of tears upon the Lord, His parents, who were bound up by the rope of affection, could not speak. They were overwhelmed, O King, and their throats choked up with tears.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Pouring out a shower of tears upon the Lord, His parents, who were bound up by the rope of affection, could not speak. They were overwhelmed, O King, and their throats choked up with tears.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The word ca indicates all the items mentioned. They were permeated internally and externally with prema which was like a rope weapon, inescapable and long. They could not understand anything (vimohitau). Tears were in their throats so they could not speak a word. All they did was stand there, showered with tears like an abhiṣeka. O king! This condition of being filled with prema can be understood by you. All this behaviour should not be seen by Nanda, since Kṛṣṇa treated both Vasudeva and Nanda as his father (but not in the other’s presence).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    They could not speak at all (ca) because their throats were choked with tears. They were completely bewildered—they could not understand anything. They showered tears and soaked Kṛṣṇa, because they were covered with ropes of prema completely (āvṛtau), which were tight and long. O king! Being naturally affectionate, you can understand this.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Prema (Divine Love)Kshama (Forgiveness / Patience)Seva (Service / Selfless Action)Atma Nigraha (Self-control)Daya (Compassion / Mercy)
    Related verses
    BG 10.4 BG 12.3 BG 16.1 BG 7.1 BG 12.2 BG 9.26 BG 4.1 BG 13.8 BG 16.4 BG 9.29 BG 6.47 BG 18.1 BG 12.1
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    The 'rope of affection' (sneha-pāśa) binding Vasudeva and Devakī recalls the rope Yaśodā tried to use on baby Kṛṣṇa — except this rope tightens around them, not around Him. He is bound by their tears because He chose to be; that is the only rope strong enough to hold Bhagavān.

    Stories
    The two ropes of Yaśodā and Devakī

    In SB 10.9, Yaśodā tries to bind Kṛṣṇa with rope after rope, each two fingers too short, until He consents. Here in Mathurā, the same Lord stands inside a different rope — sneha-pāśa, the noose of affection — and lets His parents bind Him with tears alone. Gauḍīyas connect the two scenes: dāma-bandhana in Vraja and sneha-bandhana in Mathurā are one and the same līlā of the unbindable consenting to be bound.

    Viśvanātha notes that bāṣpa-kaṇṭha (throat blocked with tears) is precisely the sāttvika-bhāva of stambha — paralysis from intensity of bhakti-rasa, listed among the eight bodily transformations of prema.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.12

    evam āśvāsya pitarau

    evam — in this way āśvāsya — assuring pitarau — His parents bhagavān — the Supreme Lord devakī - sutaḥ — the son of Devakī mātāmaham — His maternal grandfather tu — and ugrasenam — Ugrasena yadūnām — of the Yadus akarot — He made nṛpam — King .

    Important Words
    • mātāmaham — His maternal grandfather
      Mātāmaha = mother's father. Kṛṣṇa's installation of Ugrasena specifically — Devakī's father, the wronged elder Kaṁsa had imprisoned — is restorative justice in family terms first, political terms second.
    • ugrasenam — Ugrasena
      The name means 'of fierce army.' Kaṁsa's father, deposed by his own son. Restoring him is undoing Kaṁsa's primordial sin (parricide-in-effect) at its root.
    • yadūnām — of the Yadus
      The Yadu clan — Kṛṣṇa's vaṁśa. Kṛṣṇa's first political act after Kaṁsa's death is to make the Yadus a community with a head again, restoring the prajā-pālana that Kaṁsa had destroyed.
    • devakī - sutaḥ — the son of Devakī
      Kṛṣṇa is named by His mother's family in the very verse where He restores His mother's father. The vocabulary tracks the rasa: this is a daughter's-son-as-vindicator scene.
    • akarot — He made (king)
      Aorist of √kṛ — He performed the kingmaking. The Lord of all in active third-person performing rāja-sūya-tattva for someone else.
    Thus having comforted His mother and father, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, appearing as the son of Devakī, installed His maternal grandfather, Ugrasena, as King of the Yadus.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Thus having comforted His mother and father, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, appearing as the son of Devakī, installed His maternal grandfather, Ugrasena, as King of the Yadus.
    Commentary

    Krsna’s comforting Vasudeva and Devaki occurred out of the sight of Nanda. "My son tired from the fight, has retired to the inner chambers where Vasudeva and others, in great bliss, are feeding him out of affection. Since he has gone there, I will go to my own rooms since I have no fear for my son’s safety now." Saying that he went to his rooms, and therefore did not see the exchange between Krsna and Devaki and Vasudeva.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Ugrasena was qualified to be the king of the Yadus because he should be respected, being a maternal grandfather, and because he should be respected for his relation with his parents. The word tu indicates his main aim in doing this: his desire to return to Gokula. In Hari-vaṁśa Kṛṣṇa speaks to Ugrasena: ahaṁ sa eva go-madhye gopaiḥ saha vanecaraḥ / prītimān vicariṣyāmi kāmacārī yathā gajaḥ // etāvac chataśo 'py evaṁ satyena prabravīmi te / na me kāryaṁ nṛpatvena vijṣāpyaṁ kriyatām idam // bhavān rajas tu me mānyo yadūnām agraṇīḥ prabhuḥ / vijayāyābhiṣicyasva svarājye rājasattama With great affection I wander at will like an elephant among the cows along with the cowherds in Vṛndāvana. I say to you a hundred times that, in truth, I do not want to be the king. Announce this publicly. I consider that you, the best of the Yadus, should be the king and master. Best of kings! You should be consecrated in your kingdom. Kṛṣṇa made Ugrasena the king, but not as he was before, since this was done by Bhagavān: he who can do nor not do, or do otherwise. Because of the Lord’s affection for his devotee, the curse of Yayāti-- that Yadus should not be kings-- was not considered relevant. Thus previous kingship in the Yadu dynasty was actually imaginary. Ugrasena was made king after freeing him from prison. This was explained at the end of the first chapter. Viṣṇu Purāṇa says: ugrasenaṁ tato bandhān mumoca madhusūdanaḥ / abhyasiṁcat tadaivainaṁ nija-rājye hatātmajam // Kṛṣṇa freed Ugrasena from his bondage and consecrated him in his kingdom now that his son was dead. Hari-vaṁśa describes Ugrasena lamenting from Kaṁsa’s death, but it should be understood that his lamentation was only customary. This is not even considered by Śukadeva. Other facts in Hari-vamśa such as Vasudeva not being imprisoned are narrations from different kalpas.

    Stories
    saurya_epic_middle
    Related lectures
    • 13 - RGS SB 10-45-12-25 - Mathura Lila-13 - 2008 Haridwar
      His Holiness Radha Govinda Swami· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa installs His maternal grandfather Ugrasena as king the way a son who has come home for the festival hands the lamp to the elder before sitting down. Bhagavān will not Himself take Mathurā's throne — He restores it to the rightful elder and stands beside as servant.

    Stories
    Kṛṣṇa who does not take thrones

    Across the Bhāgavatam, Kṛṣṇa systematically refuses kingship for Himself: He installs Ugrasena here, Yudhiṣṭhira at Indraprastha after the rājasūya (SB 10.74), and serves as Pārtha-sārathi at Kurukṣetra. The throne He is content with is the chariot-seat, the lap, the kuñja.

    Gauḍīyas read this as the structural humility of Bhagavān: He is the Lord of all, but His līlā-form refuses public symbols of lordship. The svayam-bhagavān walks the earth as son, sārathi, sakhā, lover — never as crowned monarch.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.13

    Important Words
    • yayāti - śāpāt — because of Yayāti's curse
      Yayāti was Yadu's father and the ancestral curser (see story). Kṛṣṇa, descended in Yadu's line, accepts the bind of His own ancestor's word — line-dharma above personal preference.
    • ājñaptum arhasi — you may command
      Idiomatic — 'be so kind as to command Us.' Kṛṣṇa lowers Himself grammatically before Ugrasena; the polite imperative is the chapter's continuing thread of Bhagavān-as-junior.
    • prajāḥ — your subjects
      Prajā — those born under, citizens. Kṛṣṇa declares Himself prajā of Ugrasena. Gauḍīyas note: Bhagavān, whose every form is svarāṭ (self-effulgent ruler), willingly becomes prajā for the sake of family-order.
    • āsitavyam — should sit
      Future passive participle of √ās — that which is to be sat (on a throne). The grammatical mood (vidhi) carries the force of the curse: not 'will not' but 'must not.'
    • nṛpa - āsane — on the royal throne
      Nṛpa-āsana = king's seat. Kṛṣṇa names the precise locus the Yadu line is curse-barred from. The throne is forbidden; service (verse 14) is not.
    The Lord told him: O mighty King, We are your subjects, so please command Us. Indeed, because of the curse of Yayāti, no Yadu may sit on the royal throne.
    Purport

    Ugrasena might have told the Lord, “My dear Lord, it is actually You who should sit on the throne.” Anticipating this statement, Lord Kṛṣṇa told Ugrasena that because of Yayāti’s ancient curse, princes in the Yadu dynasty could technically not sit on the royal throne, and therefore Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma were disqualified. Of course, Ugrasena also could be considered part of the Yadu dynasty, but by the order of the Lord he could sit on the royal throne. In conclusion, these were all pastimes the Supreme Lord enjoyed as He played the part of a human being.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The Lord told him: O mighty King, We are your subjects, so please command Us. Indeed, because of the curse of Yayāti, no Yadu may sit on the royal throne.
    Commentary

    Krsna speaks to Ugrasena. "You should be the king and you should give us orders. If you say that the Yadus should not sit on the throne because of the curse of Yayati, I say, that though you are a Yadu, there is no fault if I order you to sit on the throne."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Please command us, the Yadus. “How can I order persons like you?” We are your subjects, dependent on you. The significance of word tu of the previous verse is here explained. You have dominion over the kingdom as king of the Yadus (mahā-rāja). Though you are a Yadu, you are a part of the Bhoja dynasty, whose members generally become the kings, whereas we Vṛṣṇis do not. Thus you should be the king.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    You should instruct all of us Yadus (asmān). “But they are all great persons. How can I order them?” We are you subjects, dependent on you (prajāḥ). O great king! Since you are the king of the Yadus, you control the country. Previously you had the kingdom and now you can protect us particularly. You should take complete control now. A kingdom was trifling for Kṛṣṇa and thus he did not want to rule since he desired to return to Vraja. This is described in Viṣṇu Purāṇa: N hi rājyena me kāryaṁ nāpy ahaṁ rājya-lālasaḥ Na cāpi rājya-lobhena mayākāmso nipātitaḥ Kintu loka-hitārthāya kīrtyaṁ ca sutas tava Vyaṅgabhūtaḥ kulasyāsya sānujovinipātitaḥ Ahaṁ sa eva gomadhye gopaiḥ saha vanecaraḥ Prītimān vicariyṣāmi kāmacārī yathā gajaḥ All the Yadus agreed with this skillful worlds. “Due to the curse of Yayāti, I can rule as previously, but not as the king.” Though you cannot sit on the throne as a king, you can order us as the king by my great influence. Out of shyness however he does not say this directly.

    Related lectures
    • BKG SB 10-45-13 - History of Yadu%27s curse - 2023-05-23 New Goloka
      His Holiness Bir Krishna Goswami· 2023-05-23· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa stands before His grandfather-king and says 'we are your subjects, command us' — the way an elder son of a household will let the father give the orders, even after the son has become wealthier than the father. Bhagavān keeps the form of subjecthood out of respect.

    Stories
    Yayāti's curse

    In the ninth canto (SB 9.18-19), King Yayāti, having taken his son Pūru's youth for a thousand years of enjoyment, returns it and blesses Pūru — but curses his elder son Yadu (who had refused to lend youth) that no descendant of Yadu's line shall ever sit on a royal throne. The curse stands across generations.

    When Kṛṣṇa appears in the Yadu line, He cites Yayāti's curse as the reason He cannot Himself be king. Bhagavān submits to the ancestor's word so the family-dharma is not broken. Gauḍīyas read this as deepest aiśvarya hidden inside obedience: the curser's authority is honored by the cursed line's own Bhagavān.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Important Words
    • bhṛtye — as a servant
      Bhṛtya — one supported, a servant. Bhagavān self-designates as bhṛtya. In dāsya-rasa terminology, Kṛṣṇa is here playing the role His own greatest devotees pray to play forever (cf. Hanumān, Lakṣmaṇa).
    • upāsīne — in attendance
      Locative present participle of upa + √ās — sitting near, in attendance. The same root as upāsanā (worship). Bhagavān is upāsīna (the attender) toward His grandfather — a deliberate reversal of upāsya/upāsaka roles.
    • vibudha — the demigods
      Vibudha = the awakened ones, the devas. They will bow because they know who 'the servant' really is. Kṛṣṇa's identity leaks through His service, exalting the one He serves.
    • balim haranti — will bring tribute
      Bali-haraṇa is the technical term for paying tribute to a king. Idea: the devas themselves will perform rāja-bali at Ugrasena's gate because Kṛṣṇa stands behind the throne.
    • nara - adhipāḥ — rulers of men
      Nara-adhipa = lord of men, king. 'What to speak of human kings' — kim uta. The argument is a fortiori: if devas come, mortals follow.
    Since I am present in your entourage as your personal attendant, all the demigods and other exalted personalities will come with heads bowed to offer you tribute. What, then, to speak of the rulers of men?
    Purport

    Lord Kṛṣṇa again assures Ugrasena that he should confidently take the throne.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Since I am present in your entourage as your personal attendant, all the demigods and other exalted personalities will come with heads bowed to offer you tribute. What, then, to speak of the rulers of men?
    Commentary

    If Ugrasena were to object and say that he did not have the power to be the king, Krsna says, "If I am engaged as your servant, then all the devatas will also serve you."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He shows Ugrasena’s power to command. At his command, the devatās offer tribute with heads bowed in devotion (avanatāḥ). Viṣṇu Purāṇa says: yayāti-śāpād vaṁśo ‘yam arājyārhe ‘pi sāṁpratam / mayi bhṛtye sthite devan ājṣāpayatu kiṁ nṛpaiḥ // Though by Yayātī’s curse the Yadus should not rule, while I remain as your servant, you may give orders to the devatās, what to speak of kings. Another version has hariṣyanti (will offer) instead of haranti. Anye indicates other rulers, who are inferior, or others, even kings.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Anahankarah (Freedom from False Ego)Kshama (Forgiveness / Patience)Karuna (Kindness / Compassion)

      His Holiness Bhanu Swami· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa says to Ugrasena, 'with Me as your servant, the demigods themselves will bring tribute' — the way a court official's whisper that the prince is in the room sends every petitioner to his knees. Bhagavān turns the very fact of His servanthood into the king's protection.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.45.15-16

    ↗
    सर्वान्स्वान्ज्ञतिसम्बन्धान्दिग्भ्य: कंसभयाकुलान् । यदुवृष्ण्यन्धकमधुदाशार्हकुकुरादिकान् ॥ १५ ॥ सभाजितान् समाश्वास्य विदेशावासकर्शितान् । न्यवासयत् स्वगेहेषु वित्तै: सन्तर्प्य विश्वकृत् ॥ १६ ॥

    Important Words
    • jñāti - sambandhān — close family members and relations
      Jñāti = blood-kin; sambandhi = relations by marriage and bond. The two together exhaust the Sanskrit categories of family. Kṛṣṇa recovers them all — vātsalya extending outward from parents to clan.
    • kaṁsa - bhaya - ākulān — disturbed by fear of Kaṁsa
      Three-part compound: Kaṁsa + bhaya (fear) + ākula (agitated). The Yadus had been a refugee people; Kṛṣṇa's first political work is repatriation, not conquest.
    • samāśvāsya — consoling
      Sam + ā + √śvas — to breathe deeply, to comfort. Same root as āśvāsana (consolation). Kṛṣṇa does not just resettle bodies; He breathes them back into peace.
    • videśa - āvāsa - karśitān — weary from foreign-land dwelling
      Videśa = foreign land; karśita = emaciated, worn out. Exile is named as a kṣaya (wasting). Kṛṣṇa as gṛhasthāśraya restores the missing nourishment.
    • viśva - kṛt — the maker of the universe
      He who fashioned all worlds bends to fashion the small one of clan-resettlement. Gauḍīya signature: Bhagavān's macro-power deployed for micro-care, never the reverse.
    The Lord then brought all His close family members and other relatives back from the various places to which they had fled in fear of Kaṁsa. He received the Yadus, Vṛṣṇis, Andhakas, Madhus, Dāśārhas, Kukuras and other clans with due honor, and He also consoled them, for they were weary of living in foreign lands. Then Lord Kṛṣṇa, the creator of the universe, resettled them in their homes and gratified them with valuable gifts.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The Lord then brought all His close family members and other relatives back from the various places to which they had fled in fear of Kaṁsa. He received the Yadus, Vṛṣṇis, Andhakas, Madhus, Dāśārhas, Kukuras and other clans with due honor, and He also consoled them, for they were weary of living in foreign lands. Then Lord Kṛṣṇa, the creator of the universe, resettled them in their homes and gratified them with valuable gifts.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Two verses are taken together. Kṛṣṇa brought back (this verb should be added) from various directions his direct relatives and those related through marriages (sambandhān). Or he consoled his relatives who had gone (this verb should be understood) to various directions (digbhyaḥ). Bhojas are included in the Yadus. The word ādi indicates groups like the Sātvatas. The Sātvatas and Bhojas mostly lived with Akrūra in Mathurā. Thus there were eight chief groups of Yadus: Yadus, Vṛṣṇis, Andhakas. Madhus, Dāśārhas, Kukuras, Sātvatas and Bhojas. Honouring them by quickly sending vehicles to them, he quickly (sam) brought them. They had become thin by residing in foreign lands, and, giving them abundant wealth, he established their homes by giving houses, utensils, furniture and livelihood. “How was it possible to establish living quarters for so many so quickly?” Kṛṣṇa was creator of the universe. His wealth was unobtainable by Indra. He also brought Rohiṇī from Vraja.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Many had left Mathurā and had been living in Kuru-pāṣcala and other places. Ādikān also indicates the Sātvatas. Bhojas, along with Akrūra, generally followed Kaṁsa and lived in Mathurā. These eight were the chief groups of the Yādavas. They were welcomed, quickly coming and going. They were consoled because they suffered a long time in foreign lands (karśitān). Thus they were given abundant wealth and settled quickly with celebrations, by cleaning the houses, and supplying cloth, ornaments, and furniture. How was it possible to make so many houses to be situated there? As maker of the universe, he supplied all types of wealth not available to even Indra.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Kṛṣṇa gathering the scattered Yadus, Vṛṣṇis, Andhakas, Madhus, Dāśārhas and Kukuras back from foreign lands is like a master cowherd at dusk calling the cows by name and watching the whole herd return through dust to the home gate. Bhagavān, the viśva-kṛt, gathers His own clan first.

    Important Words
    • gata - jvarāḥ — whose fever (of material life) had ceased
      Jvara = fever; gata = gone. The classical metaphor for saṁsāra-tāpa. Cure is by darśana, not by jñāna. Gauḍīyas point: bhakti operates as direct healing, not as gradual liberation.
    • mukunda - vadana - ambujam — Mukunda's lotus face
      Mukunda = giver of mukti; vadana = face; ambuja = lotus. Three nouns naming what bhakti's eye lives on. The face is not merely beautiful — it is the giver of liberation, presented daily for free.
    • labdha - manaḥ - rathāḥ — their desires fulfilled
      Manaḥ-ratha = mind-chariot, idiom for cherished wish. With Kṛṣṇa present, the mind's chariot has reached its destination — desire is fulfilled by presence, not gratification.
    • sa - daya - smita — merciful smiling
      Compound: sa-daya (with mercy) + smita (smile). Kṛṣṇa's smile is doctrinally karuṇāmaya — His mādhurya never decoupled from His daya. Rūpa Gosvāmī catalogues smita as a primary anubhāva in his rasa-śāstra.
    • kṛṣṇa - saṅkarṣaṇa - bhujaiḥ — by the arms of Kṛṣṇa and Saṅkarṣaṇa
      Bhuja = arm. The dvandva names the two-Lord protection canopy under which the city now lives. Saṅkarṣaṇa (Balarāma) is named here as protector — ādi-guru-tattva and śeṣa-tattva braided into the city's safety.
    The members of these clans, protected by the arms of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Saṅkarṣaṇa, felt that all their desires were fulfilled. Thus they enjoyed perfect happiness while living at home with their families. Because of the presence of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, they no longer suffered from the fever of material existence. Every day these loving devotees could see Mukunda’s ever-cheerful lotus face, which was decorated with beautiful, merciful smiling glances.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The members of these clans, protected by the arms of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Saṅkarṣaṇa, felt that all their desires were fulfilled. Thus they enjoyed perfect happiness while living at home with their families. Because of the presence of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, they no longer suffered from the fever of material existence. Every day these loving devotees could see Mukunda's ever-cheerful lotus face, which was decorated with beautiful, merciful smiling glances.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Three verses describe the most excellent position of the Yadus. Their affliction was finished with the killing of Kaṁsa and their desires were fulfilled by living again in Mathurā. After that they were protected by the arms of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. Then they attained all perfection on attaining the associates of Kṛṣṇa. Then they enjoyed on seeing Kṛṣṇa’s face. This is the order of the description. Saved from fear of Kaṁsa, they attained all desires by living in Mathurā with Kṛṣṇa who made his appearance there. Then they played with Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. By that they felt themselves successful (siddhāḥ). Their suffering was removed simply by the forms of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, and did not depend on anything else. Without them, everything was suffering. When they were present, there was happiness. The derivation of the names Kṛṣṇa and Saṅkarṣaṇa (attracting all) also signifies that they were able to satisfy everyone. His face is then described. They saw his face at close range (vīkṣantaḥ). And they saw it daily. They were thus pleased (prītāḥ). The happiness on seeing his face increased continually. But even then they could not be completely satisfied. The reasons are given: that face was joyful at every moment. At every moment it displayed the highest beauty. And at every moment it had merciful glances.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Now they were more attractive than Indra. They were always full of the highest happiness without any sorrow. This is expressed in two verses. They were protected from all dangers by the four arms of Kṛṣṇa, bhagavān, and his second form Balarāma, or his best servant, Ananta, the cause of the Yadu dynasty. Or “arms” is in the plural because out of affection they sometimes manifested many arms. Arms were most effective in protecting. By their strength, all their desires were fulfilled. Though living in their houses they were liberated (siddhāḥ) in their bodies. Or all their purposes were fulfilled (siddhāḥ) and they played (remire) in their houses. Though householders, they did not experience any suffering of saṁsāra at all. Krṣṇa means he who takes away all suffering. Rāma means he who gives enjoyment. Because of the two, they were without old age. They attained the highest bliss. They saw his face at close hand (vīkṣantaḥ). The present tense is used to indicate they had immediate experience of his qualities and they continually looked. They were pleased by his ever fresh qualities. Continually they saw. This was their special fortune. Or, they enjoyed (remire) continually. Or prītāḥ is the final statement. Continually his face was overjoyed, having inner joy, not receiving any offenses (pramuditam). Thus he was merciful (sadaya).

    Related values
